./validation/brainstorm-lab/openspec-music/explore-instruction.md:10:**IMPORTANT: Explore mode is for thinking, not implementing.** You may read files, search code, and investigate the codebase, but you must NEVER write code or implement features. If the user asks you to implement something, remind them to exit explore mode first and create a change proposal. You MAY create OpenSpec artifacts (proposals, designs, specs) if the user asks—that's capturing thinking, not implementing.
./validation/brainstorm-lab/openspec-music/explore-instruction.md:12:**This is a stance, not a workflow.** There are no fixed steps, no required sequence, no mandatory outputs. You're a thinking partner helping the user explore.
./validation/brainstorm-lab/openspec-music/explore-instruction.md:14:**Store selection:** If the user names a store (a store is a standalone OpenSpec repo registered on this machine) or the work lives in one, run `openspec store list --json` to discover registered store ids, then pass `--store <id>` on the commands that read or write specs and changes (`new change`, `status`, `instructions`, `list`, `show`, `validate`, `archive`, `doctor`, `context`). Other commands do not take the flag. Hints printed by commands already carry the flag; keep it on follow-ups. Without a store, commands act on the nearest local `openspec/` root.

./validation/brainstorm-lab/v3-music/apriori/runbook.md:30:**Language.** Human-facing prose — requirement docs, spec scenario descriptions, gap/design/review docs, ledger descriptions, `flow-state` notes, and every message to the human — uses the `language` field in `apriori/process-config.md`. If it is unset or `auto`, **match the language the human is using** (their kickoff and messages). Machine tokens are ALWAYS English, whatever the language: verdict lines (§5 phrase table), scenario IDs (`KV-03`), the delta keywords `ADDED`/`MODIFIED`/`REMOVED`, file paths, and this runbook. So a Chinese kickoff yields Chinese artifacts with English IDs and verdict lines — `apriori verify`/`check` keep working unchanged.
./validation/brainstorm-lab/v3-music/apriori/runbook.md:41:Follow the apriori runbook (apriori/runbook.md) for change <change-name>, tier <trivial|medium|large>, track <harden|explore> (unsure: harden).
./validation/brainstorm-lab/v3-music/apriori/runbook.md:47:> On the **harden** track, this kickoff (or the sign-off of the requirement doc) *is* the human intent acknowledgment — the intent card exists only on the **explore** track (§4). When the artifact root is externalized, the kickoff prompt must state it, because the flow-state file itself lives under it.
./validation/brainstorm-lab/v3-music/apriori/runbook.md:59:**R1 — Stop at every human gate.** The gates are: ① STEP0 verdict at round cap ② gap-report sign-off (Large tier only) ③ STEP3 technical review ④ STEP6 KB-diff approval ⑤ any cap hit or oscillation (a reopened ledger ID). The explore track (§4) adds three **named decision points** with gate status: `intent-card sign-off`, `extraction review`, `STEP2 full review`. At a gate: update the state file, report — current step, reviewer verdict lines **verbatim**, open/rejected ledger items, the decision you need — then stop. Never approve a gate yourself; never treat "the human hasn't answered" as approval.
./validation/brainstorm-lab/v3-music/apriori/runbook.md:65:**R2 — Reviews must be genuinely external.** The producing session never issues a review verdict. Spawn a heterogeneous reviewer: `codex exec -s read-only "<prompt>"` (rounds 2+: `codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <session-id> "..."` — codex CLIs ≥0.14x reject `-s` on `resume`; on older versions use `-s read-only` before the session id), or — without Codex — a **fresh** `claude` session on a different tier, fed the artifacts plus the issue ledger (P0). Paste the reviewer's verdict line back verbatim. Reviewers usually run in read-only sandboxes and cannot write the ledger: the reviewer ends its output with a **ledger delta** (new rows + status flips), and the producer lands it verbatim, marked "recorded on behalf of the reviewer"; the reviewer's raw output is archived in full at `apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*` so the recorded delta can always be diffed against its source. When invoking codex non-interactively (background/scripted), close stdin — append `< /dev/null` — or it prints "Reading additional input from stdin..." and hangs. If the reviewer dies before its verdict line lands (network/provider failure mid-review), **resume the same session** and have it finish — never fill in the verdict yourself. A read-only reviewer's **dynamic observations are untrustworthy** — test runs, builds, anything needing writes can degrade inside its sandbox and produce phantom findings; only its static reads count, and the producer rejects sandbox-artifact findings with evidence from the real environment. If you cannot actually spawn a reviewer, stop and say so — **do not simulate one**.
./validation/brainstorm-lab/v3-music/apriori/runbook.md:69:**Enforcement layers** (examples, not exhaustive; the deterministic items below are *available to configure*, not active by default in this repo). Advisory text gets ignored under pressure — classify each rule by how it can be enforced: ① **deterministically enforceable now** — `process-config.md` read-only (a hook blocking agent writes), `apriori check` as a required pre-commit/CI check, `apriori verify` as the STEP5 binding gate, and the **verdict-evidence check**: every verdict line must have a matching raw archive file (`apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*`) — a mechanical backstop against simulated reviews; ② **gate-level** — Stop hooks and `/goal` conditions; ③ **inherently advisory** — a reviewer's independent judgment quality, semantic adherence to the P prompts. Reference implementation is Claude Code hooks; any CI can enforce the same checks. Example (a PreToolUse hook blocking config writes — illustrative sketch; exact schema in the Claude Code hooks docs):

./validation/brainstorm-lab/v3-music/apriori/runbook.md:178:**Funnel — the human decides, and the fire is carried.** "Stateable" is the human's judgment, not yours: after the approaches comparison you may *propose* exiting; only the human's approval ends the stance — and it **must funnel into the pipeline**. On approval of a stateable goal, start **STEP0**; if the goal still cannot be stated, route to the **explore track's intent card** (§4) — the same goal-certainty split as §2. There is no third resting place: brainstorm feeds one of the two. On funnel, carry everything: write the crystallized understanding as the kickoff requirement draft — goal, users, chosen approach (and the UI sketch that won, if any), constraints, non-goals, open questions — which becomes STEP0's `req-v1` starting material. Brainstorm never replaces STEP0's requirement discipline — it feeds it.

./validation/brainstorm-lab2/v3-organizer/apriori/runbook.md:30:**Language.** Human-facing prose — requirement docs, spec scenario descriptions, gap/design/review docs, ledger descriptions, `flow-state` notes, and every message to the human — uses the `language` field in `apriori/process-config.md`. If it is unset or `auto`, **match the language the human is using** (their kickoff and messages). ./validation/brainstorm-lab2/v3-organizer/apriori/runbook.md:47:> On the **harden** track, this kickoff (or the sign-off of the requirement doc) *is* the human intent acknowledgment — the intent card exists only on the **explore** track (§4). ./validation/brainstorm-lab2/v3-organizer/apriori/runbook.md:59:**R1 — Stop at every human gate.** The gates are: ① STEP0 verdict at round cap ② gap-report sign-off (Large tier only) ③ STEP3 technical review ④ STEP6 KB-diff approval ⑤ any cap hit or oscillation (a reopened ledger ID). The explore track (§4) adds three **named decision points** with gate status: `intent-card sign-off`, `extraction review`, `STEP2 full review`. At a gate: update the state file, report — current step, reviewer verdict lines **verbatim**, open/rejected ledger items, the decision you need — then stop. Never approve a gate yourself; never treat "the human hasn't answered" as approval.
./validation/brainstorm-lab2/v3-organizer/apriori/runbook.md:65:**R2 — Reviews must be genuinely external.** The producing session never issues a review verdict. Spawn a heterogeneous reviewer: `codex exec -s read-only "<prompt>"` (rounds 2+: `codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <session-id> "..."` — codex CLIs ≥0.14x reject `-s` on `resume`; on older versions use `-s read-only` before the session id), or — without Codex — a **fresh** `claude` session on a different tier, fed the artifacts plus the issue ledger (P0). Paste the reviewer's verdict line back verbatim. Reviewers usually run in read-only sandboxes and cannot write the ledger: the reviewer ends its output with a **ledger delta** (new rows + status flips), and the producer lands it verbatim, marked "recorded on behalf of the reviewer"; the reviewer's raw output is archived in full at `apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*` so the recorded delta can always be diffed against its source. When invoking codex non-interactively (background/scripted), close stdin — append `< /dev/null` — or it prints "Reading additional input from stdin..." and hangs. If the reviewer dies before its verdict line lands (network/provider failure mid-review), **resume the same session** and have it finish — never fill in the verdict yourself. For this to survive a *producer*-side interruption too, record the reviewer's session id in flow-state's `reviewer-session` field the moment round 1 prints it — otherwise a resume after a crash has no session to reattach to. A read-only reviewer's **dynamic observations are untrustworthy** — test runs, builds, anything needing writes can degrade inside its sandbox and produce phantom findings; only its static reads count, and the producer rejects sandbox-artifact findings with evidence from the real environment. If you cannot actually spawn a reviewer, stop and say so — **do not simulate one**.
./validation/brainstorm-lab2/v3-organizer/apriori/runbook.md:69:**Enforcement layers** (examples, not exhaustive; the deterministic items below are *available to configure*, not active by default in this repo). Advisory text gets ignored under pressure — classify each rule by how it can be enforced: ① **deterministically enforceable now** — `process-config.md` read-only (a hook blocking agent writes), `apriori check` as a required pre-commit/CI check, `apriori verify` as the STEP5 binding gate, and the **verdict-evidence check**: every verdict line must have a matching raw archive file (`apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*`) — a mechanical backstop against simulated reviews; ② **gate-level** — Stop hooks and `/goal` conditions; ③ **inherently advisory** — a reviewer's independent judgment quality, semantic adherence to the P prompts. Reference implementation is Claude Code hooks; any CI can enforce the same checks. Example (a PreToolUse hook blocking config writes — illustrative sketch; exact schema in the Claude Code hooks docs):

./validation/brainstorm-lab2/v3-organizer/apriori/runbook.md:185:**Funnel — the human decides, and the fire is carried.** "Stateable" is the human's judgment, not yours: after the approaches comparison you may *propose* exiting; only the human's approval ends the stance — and it **must funnel into the pipeline**. On approval of a stateable goal, start **STEP0**; if the goal still cannot be stated, route to the **explore track's intent card** (§4) — the same goal-certainty split as §2. There is no third resting place: brainstorm feeds one of the two. On funnel, carry everything: write the crystallized understanding as the kickoff requirement draft — goal, users, chosen approach (and the UI sketch that won, if any), success criteria, constraints, non-goals **with the reasons they were cut**, open questions — which becomes STEP0's `req-v1` starting material. Brainstorm never replaces STEP0's requirement discipline — it feeds it.

./validation/brainstorm-lab2/openspec-organizer/explore-instruction.md:10:**IMPORTANT: Explore mode is for thinking, not implementing.** You may read files, search code, and investigate the codebase, but you must NEVER write code or implement features. If the user asks you to implement something, remind them to exit explore mode first and create a change proposal. You MAY create OpenSpec artifacts (proposals, designs, specs) if the user asks—that's capturing thinking, not implementing.
./validation/brainstorm-lab2/openspec-organizer/explore-instruction.md:12:**This is a stance, not a workflow.** There are no fixed steps, no required sequence, no mandatory outputs. You're a thinking partner helping the user explore.
./validation/brainstorm-lab2/openspec-organizer/explore-instruction.md:14:**Store selection:** If the user names a store (a store is a standalone OpenSpec repo registered on this machine) or the work lives in one, run `openspec store list --json` to discover registered store ids, then pass `--store <id>` on the commands that read or write specs and changes (`new change`, `status`, `instructions`, `list`, `show`, `validate`, `archive`, `doctor`, `context`). Other commands do not take the flag. Hints printed by commands already carry the flag; keep it on follow-ups. Without a store, commands act on the nearest local `openspec/` root.

./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3.txt:2230:./apriori/design/quick-poll-review-v1.md:21:- **Suggestion**: 明确 queue 实现：以前序 `catch(() => {})` 后的 resolved tail 执行任务；返回当前任务结果；并在 `finally` 中只清理当前 tail，避免 Map 泄漏和竞态删除。

./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3.txt:2344:./apriori/runbook.md:59:**R1 — Stop at every human gate.** The gates are: ① STEP0 verdict at round cap ② gap-report sign-off (Large tier only) ③ STEP3 technical review ④ STEP6 KB-diff approval ⑤ any cap hit or oscillation (a reopened ledger ID). The explore track (§4) adds three **named decision points** with gate status: `intent-card sign-off`, `extraction review`, `STEP2 full review`. At a gate: update the state file, report — current step, reviewer verdict lines **verbatim**, open/rejected ledger items, the decision you need — then stop. Never approve a gate yourself; never treat "the human hasn't answered" as approval.
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3.txt:2345:./apriori/runbook.md:65:**R2 — Reviews must be genuinely external.** The producing session never issues a review verdict. Spawn a heterogeneous reviewer: `codex exec -s read-only "<prompt>"` (rounds 2+: `codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <session-id> "..."` — codex CLIs ≥0.14x reject `-s` on `resume`; on older versions use `-s read-only` before the session id), or — without Codex — a **fresh** `claude` session on a different tier, fed the artifacts plus the issue ledger (P0). Paste the reviewer's verdict line back verbatim. Reviewers usually run in read-only sandboxes and cannot write the ledger: the reviewer ends its output with a **ledger delta** (new rows + status flips), and the producer lands it verbatim, marked "recorded on behalf of the reviewer"; the reviewer's raw output is archived in full at `apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*` so the recorded delta can always be diffed against its source. When invoking codex non-interactively (background/scripted), close stdin — append `< /dev/null` — or it prints "Reading additional input from stdin..." and hangs. If the reviewer dies before its verdict line lands (network/provider failure mid-review), **resume the same session** and have it finish — never fill in the verdict yourself. A read-only reviewer's **dynamic observations are untrustworthy** — test runs, builds, anything needing writes can degrade inside its sandbox and produce phantom findings; only its static reads count, and the producer rejects sandbox-artifact findings with evidence from the real environment. If you cannot actually spawn a reviewer, stop and say so — **do not simulate one**.
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3.txt:2346:./apriori/runbook.md:69:**Enforcement layers** (examples, not exhaustive; the deterministic items below are *available to configure*, not active by default in this repo). Advisory text gets ignored under pressure — classify each rule by how it can be enforced: ① **deterministically enforceable now** — `process-config.md` read-only (a hook blocking agent writes), `apriori check` as a required pre-commit/CI check, `apriori verify` as the STEP5 binding gate, and the **verdict-evidence check**: every verdict line must have a matching raw archive file (`apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*`) — a mechanical backstop against simulated reviews; ② **gate-level** — Stop hooks and `/goal` conditions; ③ **inherently advisory** — a reviewer's independent judgment quality, semantic adherence to the P prompts. Reference implementation is Claude Code hooks; any CI can enforce the same checks. Example (a PreToolUse hook blocking config writes — illustrative sketch; exact schema in the Claude Code hooks docs):
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3.txt:2347:./apriori/runbook.md:188:0. **Intent card first (non-waivable):** ≤15 lines at `requirement/intent-card.md` — goal hypothesis / success criteria / the questions the spike must answer. Requires **human sign-off** (`intent-card sign-off`; a heterogeneous review may inform it, but cannot replace it). On this track the intent card is the independent review baseline — the extracted spec is never judged against the prototype alone.

./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:989:    30	**Language.** Human-facing prose — requirement docs, spec scenario descriptions, gap/design/review docs, ledger descriptions, `flow-state` notes, and every message to the human — uses the `language` field in `apriori/process-config.md`. If it is unset or `auto`, **match the language the human is using** (their kickoff and messages). Machine tokens are ALWAYS English, whatever the language: verdict lines (§5 phrase table), scenario IDs (`KV-03`), the delta keywords `ADDED`/`MODIFIED`/`REMOVED`, file paths, and this runbook. So a Chinese kickoff yields Chinese artifacts with English IDs and verdict lines — `apriori verify`/`check` keep working unchanged.

./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:1006:    47	> On the **harden** track, this kickoff (or the sign-off of the requirement doc) *is* the human intent acknowledgment — the intent card exists only on the **explore** track (§4). When the artifact root is externalized, the kickoff prompt must state it, because the flow-state file itself lives under it.
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:1018:    59	**R1 — Stop at every human gate.** The gates are: ① STEP0 verdict at round cap ② gap-report sign-off (Large tier only) ③ STEP3 technical review ④ STEP6 KB-diff approval ⑤ any cap hit or oscillation (a reopened ledger ID). The explore track (§4) adds three **named decision points** with gate status: `intent-card sign-off`, `extraction review`, `STEP2 full review`. At a gate: update the state file, report — current step, reviewer verdict lines **verbatim**, open/rejected ledger items, the decision you need — then stop. Never approve a gate yourself; never treat "the human hasn't answered" as approval.
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:1024:    65	**R2 — Reviews must be genuinely external.** The producing session never issues a review verdict. Spawn a heterogeneous reviewer: `codex exec -s read-only "<prompt>"` (rounds 2+: `codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <session-id> "..."` — codex CLIs ≥0.14x reject `-s` on `resume`; on older versions use `-s read-only` before the session id), or — without Codex — a **fresh** `claude` session on a different tier, fed the artifacts plus the issue ledger (P0). Paste the reviewer's verdict line back verbatim. Reviewers usually run in read-only sandboxes and cannot write the ledger: the reviewer ends its output with a **ledger delta** (new rows + status flips), and the producer lands it verbatim, marked "recorded on behalf of the reviewer"; the reviewer's raw output is archived in full at `apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*` so the recorded delta can always be diffed against its source. When invoking codex non-interactively (background/scripted), close stdin — append `< /dev/null` — or it prints "Reading additional input from stdin..." and hangs. If the reviewer dies before its verdict line lands (network/provider failure mid-review), **resume the same session** and have it finish — never fill in the verdict yourself. For this to survive a *producer*-side interruption too, record the reviewer's session id in flow-state's `reviewer-session` field the moment round 1 prints it — otherwise a resume after a crash has no session to reattach to. A read-only reviewer's **dynamic observations are untrustworthy** — test runs, builds, anything needing writes can degrade inside its sandbox and produce phantom findings; only its static reads count, and the producer rejects sandbox-artifact findings with evidence from the real environment. If you cannot actually spawn a reviewer, stop and say so — **do not simulate one**.
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:1028:    69	**Enforcement layers** (examples, not exhaustive; the deterministic items below are *available to configure*, not active by default in this repo). Advisory text gets ignored under pressure — classify each rule by how it can be enforced: ① **deterministically enforceable now** — `process-config.md` read-only (a hook blocking agent writes), `apriori check` as a required pre-commit/CI check, `apriori verify` as the STEP5 binding gate, and the **verdict-evidence check**: every verdict line must have a matching raw archive file (`apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*`) — a mechanical backstop against simulated reviews; ② **gate-level** — Stop hooks and `/goal` conditions; ③ **inherently advisory** — a reviewer's independent judgment quality, semantic adherence to the P prompts. Reference implementation is Claude Code hooks; any CI can enforce the same checks. Example (a PreToolUse hook blocking config writes — illustrative sketch; exact schema in the Claude Code hooks docs):

./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:1144:   185	**Funnel — the human decides, and the fire is carried.** "Stateable" is the human's judgment, not yours: after the approaches comparison you may *propose* exiting; only the human's approval ends the stance — and it **must funnel into the pipeline**. On approval of a stateable goal, start **STEP0**; if the goal still cannot be stated, route to the **explore track's intent card** (§4) — the same goal-certainty split as §2. There is no third resting place: brainstorm feeds one of the two. On funnel, carry everything: write the crystallized understanding as the kickoff requirement draft — goal, users, chosen approach (and the UI sketch that won, if any), success criteria, constraints, non-goals **with the reasons they were cut**, open questions — which becomes STEP0's `req-v1` starting material. Brainstorm never replaces STEP0's requirement discipline — it feeds it.

./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:2867:requirement/req-final.md:69:  - **提交点之前**(临时文件写/`fsync(临时文件)`、`rename` 本身)任一失败 → 返回 `500 PERSIST_FAILED`,**旧文件保持不变、无任何可观测变更**(内存计数不对外可见)。这条保证——"`500` ⇒ 无可观测变更"——始终成立。
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:2868:requirement/req-final.md:70:  - **提交点之后**的 `fsync(目录)` 属崩溃持久性硬化:若它失败,`rename` 已提交、数据已可见,则**提交成立、返回 2xx 并记录持久性告警**(不得谎报 `500`,否则违反上一条);该单次写在紧接掉电下有极小的失同步残余风险。
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:2869:requirement/req-final.md:71:  - 因此:**2xx = 已提交且可见**;**跨崩溃持久性在 `fsync(目录)` 成功的正常路径下成立**(这是被 CC-02 kill-after-ack 测试验证的路径)。

./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:3027:./apriori/runbook.md:65:**R2 — Reviews must be genuinely external.** The producing session never issues a review verdict. Spawn a heterogeneous reviewer: `codex exec -s read-only "<prompt>"` (rounds 2+: `codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <session-id> "..."` — codex CLIs ≥0.14x reject `-s` on `resume`; on older versions use `-s read-only` before the session id), or — without Codex — a **fresh** `claude` session on a different tier, fed the artifacts plus the issue ledger (P0). Paste the reviewer's verdict line back verbatim. Reviewers usually run in read-only sandboxes and cannot write the ledger: the reviewer ends its output with a **ledger delta** (new rows + status flips), and the producer lands it verbatim, marked "recorded on behalf of the reviewer"; the reviewer's raw output is archived in full at `apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*` so the recorded delta can always be diffed against its source. When invoking codex non-interactively (background/scripted), close stdin — append `< /dev/null` — or it prints "Reading additional input from stdin..." and hangs. If the reviewer dies before its verdict line lands (network/provider failure mid-review), **resume the same session** and have it finish — never fill in the verdict yourself. For this to survive a *producer*-side interruption too, record the reviewer's session id in flow-state's `reviewer-session` field the moment round 1 prints it — otherwise a resume after a crash has no session to reattach to. A read-only reviewer's **dynamic observations are untrustworthy** — test runs, builds, anything needing writes can degrade inside its sandbox and produce phantom findings; only its static reads count, and the producer rejects sandbox-artifact findings with evidence from the real environment. If you cannot actually spawn a reviewer, stop and say so — **do not simulate one**.

./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:3204:./apriori/review/quick-poll-step5-review-v2-raw.txt:2089:GAP-5: open. The original “500 after visible rename” bug is removed, but the replacement commit-point semantics are not yet coherent with the still-active durability contract. `atomicWrite` now returns success after a directory fsync failure (`src/store.js:53-63`), and CC-05 explicitly accepts a residual crash-durability risk after such a 200 (`apriori/changes/quick-poll/specs/quick-poll.md:199-201`). That contradicts CC-02’s unqualified guarantee that a 2xx-acked vote survives immediate `SIGKILL` and says success means persisted with temp+directory fsync (`apriori/changes/quick-poll/specs/quick-poll.md:187-189`), and also contradicts `req-final` §8, which still says any failure in temp fsync, rename, or directory fsync returns `500 PERSIST_FAILED` (`requirement/req-final.md:67-68`).  
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:3205:./apriori/review/quick-poll-step5-review-v2-raw.txt:2090:Suggested fix: either keep the hard crash-durability contract and define a post-rename fsync failure handling strategy that does not return ordinary success, or formally weaken/update req-final and CC-02 so 2xx means “rename committed and visible,” not “directory-fsynced crash durable,” with CC-02 scoped to the successful fsync path.

./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:3222:./apriori/review/quick-poll-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:510:- **runtime update(运行时更新)**:每次 vote/close 经 per-poll 队列串行:`load → 校验/改内存 → 原子落盘`。原子落盘 = 写 `<id>.json.tmp.<rand>` → `fsync(tmpFd)` → `rename` 覆盖 → `fsync(dirFd)`;任一步失败抛错 → 该请求 500,内存改动丢弃(不对外可见)。
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:3223:./apriori/review/quick-poll-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:511:- **cleanup / invalidation(清理/失效)**:无自动清理(long-lived,YAGNI)。失效即"逻辑关闭":`status="closed"` 或 `now≥deadline`;deadline 过期在首次 load 时 lazy 持久化为 closed(CL-06)。启动读到损坏 JSON → 记日志跳过,不崩溃。
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:3224:./apriori/review/quick-poll-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:516:- **cleanup / invalidation**:链尾 settle 后从 map 删除已完成条目(避免无限增长);进程重启即全清(队列是易失的,真值在磁盘)。
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:3225:./apriori/review/quick-poll-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:518:> 单进程模型下磁盘文件的唯一写者是本进程,进程内队列即足以串行化;不引入跨进程锁(非目标)。若未来多进程部署 → 升级为文件锁/外部锁,记为 KB Decision。
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r3.txt:3226:./apriori/review/quick-poll-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:523:- **崩溃持久化(CC-02)**:2xx 仅在 `rename` 且 `fsync(dir)` 成功后返回;fsync 覆盖文件与父目录 → 崩溃后 rename 已落盘。经典陷阱:只 fsync 临时文件不 fsync 目录会在崩溃后丢失 rename —— 本设计两者都 fsync。

./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r2.txt:1459:requirement/req-v2.md:43:- **PC-02 创建输入校验**：违反 §3/§4 任一校验（空标题/超长、选项<2 或>20、空选项、非法 mode、malformed 或过去 deadline）→ 拒绝创建，HTTP 非 2xx，可读错误，**不创建任何投票记录**。
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r2.txt:1460:requirement/req-v2.md:49:- **PC-05 浏览器软限重复投票**：同一浏览器对同一投票默认只记一次；成功投票（2xx）后本地置标记；再次打开投票链接 → 直接进入结果视图。标记存于浏览器 **localStorage**，key 作用域为 `origin + pollId`；换设备/清缓存/无痕不受本工具阻止（刻意的零门槛取舍）。
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r2.txt:1461:requirement/req-v2.md:53:- **PC-06 投完即看实时结果**：提交成功后进入结果视图，显示各选项票数与总票数；结果页**加载即拉取一次**，此后每 **3000ms**（`POLL_INTERVAL_MS` 常量，可配置）自动拉取最新结果（轮询，非推送）。结果 endpoint 返回 `{title, options:[{id,text,count}], total, status, deadline}`。
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r2.txt:1462:requirement/req-v2.md:56:- **PC-07 到点自动关闭**：设了 deadline 的投票，当 `Date.now() >= deadline` → 视为「已关闭」；此后投票提交返回非 2xx 并提示已结束，结果仍可读。
./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r2.txt:1463:requirement/req-v2.md:71:- PC-05：同一浏览器已投后再次访问投票链接 → 直接结果视图（localStorage 标记，作用域 origin+pollId）。

./validation/fullrun-lab/audit-v3-r2.txt:1465:requirement/req-v2.md:83:- **初始化**：创建投票时生成不可枚举 `pollId` + `adminKey`（CSPRNG），落一个投票数据文件（title、options[含 id]、mode、deadline(epoch ms)、counts=0、status=open）。

./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r3/apriori/runbook.md:30:**Language.** Human-facing prose — requirement docs, spec scenario descriptions, gap/design/review docs, ledger descriptions, `flow-state` notes, and every message to the human — uses the `language` field in `apriori/process-config.md`. If it is unset or `auto`, **match the language the human is using** (their kickoff and messages). Machine tokens are ALWAYS English, whatever the language: verdict lines (§5 phrase table), scenario IDs (`KV-03`), the delta keywords `ADDED`/`MODIFIED`/`REMOVED`, file paths, and this runbook. So a Chinese kickoff yields Chinese artifacts with English IDs and verdict lines — `apriori verify`/`check` keep working unchanged.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r3/apriori/runbook.md:41:Follow the apriori runbook (apriori/runbook.md) for change <change-name>, tier <trivial|medium|large>, track <harden|explore> (unsure: harden).
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r3/apriori/runbook.md:47:> On the **harden** track, this kickoff (or the sign-off of the requirement doc) *is* the human intent acknowledgment — the intent card exists only on the **explore** track (§4). When the artifact root is externalized, the kickoff prompt must state it, because the flow-state file itself lives under it.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r3/apriori/runbook.md:59:**R1 — Stop at every human gate.** The gates are: ① STEP0 verdict at round cap ② gap-report sign-off (Large tier only) ③ STEP3 technical review ④ STEP6 KB-diff approval ⑤ any cap hit or oscillation (a reopened ledger ID). The explore track (§4) adds three **named decision points** with gate status: `intent-card sign-off`, `extraction review`, `STEP2 full review`. At a gate: update the state file, report — current step, reviewer verdict lines **verbatim**, open/rejected ledger items, the decision you need — then stop. Never approve a gate yourself; never treat "the human hasn't answered" as approval.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r3/apriori/runbook.md:65:**R2 — Reviews must be genuinely external.** The producing session never issues a review verdict. Spawn a heterogeneous reviewer: `codex exec -s read-only "<prompt>"` (rounds 2+: `codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <session-id> "..."` — codex CLIs ≥0.14x reject `-s` on `resume`; on older versions use `-s read-only` before the session id), or — without Codex — a **fresh** `claude` session on a different tier, fed the artifacts plus the issue ledger (P0). Paste the reviewer's verdict line back verbatim. Reviewers usually run in read-only sandboxes and cannot write the ledger: the reviewer ends its output with a **ledger delta** (new rows + status flips), and the producer lands it verbatim, marked "recorded on behalf of the reviewer"; the reviewer's raw output is archived in full at `apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*` so the recorded delta can always be diffed against its source. When invoking codex non-interactively (background/scripted), close stdin — append `< /dev/null` — or it prints "Reading additional input from stdin..." and hangs. If the reviewer dies before its verdict line lands (network/provider failure mid-review), **resume the same session** and have it finish — never fill in the verdict yourself. For this to survive a *producer*-side interruption too, record the reviewer's session id in flow-state's `reviewer-session` field the moment round 1 prints it — otherwise a resume after a crash has no session to reattach to. A read-only reviewer's **dynamic observations are untrustworthy** — test runs, builds, anything needing writes can degrade inside its sandbox and produce phantom findings; only its static reads count, and the producer rejects sandbox-artifact findings with evidence from the real environment. If you cannot actually spawn a reviewer, stop and say so — **do not simulate one**.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r3/apriori/runbook.md:69:**Enforcement layers** (examples, not exhaustive; the deterministic items below are *available to configure*, not active by default in this repo). Advisory text gets ignored under pressure — classify each rule by how it can be enforced: ① **deterministically enforceable now** — `process-config.md` read-only (a hook blocking agent writes), `apriori check` as a required pre-commit/CI check, `apriori verify` as the STEP5 binding gate, and the **verdict-evidence check**: every verdict line must have a matching raw archive file (`apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*`) — a mechanical backstop against simulated reviews; ② **gate-level** — Stop hooks and `/goal` conditions; ③ **inherently advisory** — a reviewer's independent judgment quality, semantic adherence to the P prompts. Reference implementation is Claude Code hooks; any CI can enforce the same checks. Example (a PreToolUse hook blocking config writes — illustrative sketch; exact schema in the Claude Code hooks docs):

Default when unsure: **harden** — the opposite direction from the size axis, because the risks point the opposite way.

./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r3/apriori/runbook.md:185:**Funnel — the human decides, and the fire is carried.** "Stateable" is the human's judgment, not yours: after the approaches comparison you may *propose* exiting; only the human's approval ends the stance — and it **must funnel into the pipeline**. On approval of a stateable goal, start **STEP0**; if the goal still cannot be stated, route to the **explore track's intent card** (§4) — the same goal-certainty split as §2. There is no third resting place: brainstorm feeds one of the two. On funnel, carry everything: write the crystallized understanding as the kickoff requirement draft — goal, users, chosen approach (and the UI sketch that won, if any), success criteria, constraints, non-goals **with the reasons they were cut**, open questions — which becomes STEP0's `req-v1` starting material. Brainstorm never replaces STEP0's requirement discipline — it feeds it.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r3/apriori/runbook.md:189:- **In:** `requirement/req-v{N}.md`; KB if any. The requirement must state its **target lineage** (mainline / which branch line) — in multi-lineage repos a missing lineage is a fourth interview trigger. If the requirement lacks any of the three essentials — goal / out-of-scope / testable acceptance — **interview the human first** with structured questions, then draft req-v1.

./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/runbook.md:30:**Language.** Human-facing prose — requirement docs, spec scenario descriptions, gap/design/review docs, ledger descriptions, `flow-state` notes, and every message to the human — uses the `language` field in `apriori/process-config.md`. If it is unset or `auto`, **match the language the human is using** (their kickoff and messages). Machine tokens are ALWAYS English, whatever the language: verdict lines (§5 phrase table), scenario IDs (`KV-03`), the delta keywords `ADDED`/`MODIFIED`/`REMOVED`, file paths, and this runbook. So a Chinese kickoff yields Chinese artifacts with English IDs and verdict lines — `apriori verify`/`check` keep working unchanged.
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/runbook.md:43:Follow the apriori runbook (apriori/runbook.md) for change <change-name>, tier <trivial|medium|large>, track <harden|explore> (unsure: harden).
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/runbook.md:49:> On the **harden** track, this kickoff (or the sign-off of the requirement doc) *is* the human intent acknowledgment — the intent card exists only on the **explore** track (§4). When the artifact root is externalized, the kickoff prompt must state it, because the flow-state file itself lives under it.
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/runbook.md:61:**R1 — Stop at every human gate.** The gates are: ① STEP0 verdict at round cap ② gap-report sign-off (Large tier only) ③ STEP3 technical review ④ STEP6 KB-diff approval ⑤ any cap hit or oscillation (a reopened ledger ID). The explore track (§4) adds three **named decision points** with gate status: `intent-card sign-off`, `extraction review`, `STEP2 full review`. At a gate: update the state file, report — current step, reviewer verdict lines **verbatim**, open/rejected ledger items, the decision you need — then stop. Never approve a gate yourself; never treat "the human hasn't answered" as approval.
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/runbook.md:67:**R2 — Reviews must be genuinely external.** The producing session never issues a review verdict. Spawn a heterogeneous reviewer: `codex exec -s read-only "<prompt>"` (rounds 2+: `codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <session-id> "..."` — codex CLIs ≥0.14x reject `-s` on `resume`; on older versions use `-s read-only` before the session id), or — without Codex — a **fresh** `claude` session on a different tier, fed the artifacts plus the issue ledger (P0). Paste the reviewer's verdict line back verbatim. Reviewers usually run in read-only sandboxes and cannot write the ledger: the reviewer ends its output with a **ledger delta** (new rows + status flips), and the producer lands it verbatim, marked "recorded on behalf of the reviewer"; the reviewer's raw output is archived in full at `apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*` so the recorded delta can always be diffed against its source. When invoking codex non-interactively (background/scripted), close stdin — append `< /dev/null` (PowerShell has no /dev/null: pipe instead, `$null | codex exec …`) — or it prints "Reading additional input from stdin..." and hangs. If the reviewer dies before its verdict line lands (network/provider failure mid-review), **resume the same session** and have it finish — never fill in the verdict yourself. For this to survive a *producer*-side interruption too, record the reviewer's session id in flow-state's `reviewer-session` field the moment round 1 prints it — otherwise a resume after a crash has no session to reattach to. A read-only reviewer's **dynamic observations are untrustworthy** — test runs, builds, anything needing writes can degrade inside its sandbox and produce phantom findings; only its static reads count, and the producer rejects sandbox-artifact findings with evidence from the real environment. If you cannot actually spawn a reviewer, stop and say so — **do not simulate one**.
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/runbook.md:71:**Enforcement layers** (examples, not exhaustive; the deterministic items below are *available to configure*, not active by default in this repo). Advisory text gets ignored under pressure — classify each rule by how it can be enforced: ① **deterministically enforceable now** — `process-config.md` read-only (a hook blocking agent writes), `apriori check` as a required pre-commit/CI check, `apriori verify` as the STEP5 binding gate, and the **verdict-evidence check**: every verdict line must have a matching raw archive file (`apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*`) — a mechanical backstop against simulated reviews; ② **gate-level** — Stop hooks and `/goal` conditions; ③ **inherently advisory** — a reviewer's independent judgment quality, semantic adherence to the P prompts. Reference implementation is Claude Code hooks; any CI can enforce the same checks. Example (a PreToolUse hook blocking config writes — illustrative sketch; exact schema in the Claude Code hooks docs):

./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/runbook.md:96:| Neither the goal nor its acceptance can be stated | **explore** (§4's explore track) |

Default when unsure: **harden** — the opposite direction from the size axis, because the risks point the opposite way.

./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/runbook.md:187:**Funnel — the human decides, and the fire is carried.** "Stateable" is the human's judgment, not yours: after the approaches comparison you may *propose* exiting; only the human's approval ends the stance — and it **must funnel into the pipeline**. On approval of a stateable goal, start **STEP0**; if the goal still cannot be stated, route to the **explore track's intent card** (§4) — the same goal-certainty split as §2. There is no third resting place: brainstorm feeds one of the two. On funnel, carry everything: write the crystallized understanding as the kickoff requirement draft — goal, users, chosen approach (and the UI sketch that won, if any), success criteria, constraints, non-goals **with the reasons they were cut**, open questions — which becomes STEP0's `req-v1` starting material. Brainstorm never replaces STEP0's requirement discipline — it feeds it.
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/runbook.md:191:- **In:** `requirement/req-v{N}.md`; KB if any. The requirement must state its **target lineage** (mainline / which branch line) — in multi-lineage repos a missing lineage is a fourth interview trigger. ./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2154:requirement/req-final.md:1:# req-v2 — vote-dedup：服务端防重复投票（cookie 级别）
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2155:requirement/req-final.md:11:当前"防重复投票"只存在于前端：`poll.html` 投票成功后写 `localStorage['voted:'+id]`，仅用于隐藏表单。服务端 `vote()` 对调用者身份零感知——同一请求用 curl 原样重放，每次都 200 并计票（已实测：3 次重放 → total 1→2→3）。服务端目前没有任何 cookie 的读写代码。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2156:requirement/req-final.md:15:服务端以**匿名、不可伪造的投票者标识**（经 HttpOnly cookie 承载）为准，拒绝同一标识对同一投票的重复投票。不引入登录/账号体系。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2157:requirement/req-final.md:19:- **B1 标识下发**：客户端请求 `GET /api/polls/:id` 时，若请求未带有效标识 cookie，服务端生成匿名投票者标识并经 `Set-Cookie` 下发；已带有效标识的请求不重复下发。标识全站一个（不按 poll 区分）。`POST /api/polls/:id/vote` **成功响应（200）** 时同样确保调用方带着（或已被下发过）有效标识——即成功投票必然对应一个已下发的标识,不会出现"投中了但从未 Set-Cookie"的情形；**失败响应（400/404/409）不下发新 cookie**（不奖励非法请求）。`GET /poll/:id`、`GET /admin/:token`、`GET /`、`GET /style.css` 等页面/静态路由**不下发、不读取**该 cookie——签发入口只在 API 层，页面加载后必然会调用 `GET /api/polls/:id`，行为等价，实现集中在一处。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2158:requirement/req-final.md:20:  - cookie 名称：`pv`（poll voter，避免暴露实现细节）。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2159:requirement/req-final.md:22:- **B2 标识不可伪造**：标识格式为 `<random>.<hmac-sha256 签名, base64url>`；`random` 为 `crypto.randomBytes(16)` 的 base64url 编码。签名密钥（32 字节随机）持久化于 `<dataDir>/cookie-secret`（与 `polls.json` 同目录，权限依赖操作系统默认，不额外加固——与本项目现有"零额外基础设施"风格一致）：
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2160:requirement/req-final.md:25:  - 密钥文件存在但**内容不是合法长度**（读取后长度不等于预期字节数）→ 视为损坏：**服务启动失败**（抛出错误，非静默重建）——静默重建会让所有既有 cookie 静默失效且无操作留痕，優先选择显式失败，由运维介入；这与该文件已损坏时"重建更省事但吞掉一次隐性数据丢失"的取舍相反，选择更保守的一侧。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2161:requirement/req-final.md:26:  - 校验失败（签名不匹配、格式不符）的 cookie 值，在 GET 路径按"无标识"处理（视为新访客，可能触发重新下发）；在 POST 路径按"无有效标识"处理（见 B3，拒绝）。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2162:requirement/req-final.md:27:- **B3 投票必须携带有效标识**：`POST /api/polls/:id/vote` 无 cookie 或标识非法（格式错误/签名不匹配）→ `403` + `{error:"未获得投票标识"}`，**不计票**，**不下发新 cookie**（见 B1）。正常浏览器用户不受影响（打开页面即已通过一次 GET 领到标识）。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2163:requirement/req-final.md:29:  - 重复投票 → `409` + `{error:"你已经投过这个投票了"}`，票数不变。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2164:requirement/req-final.md:31:  - 若 `store.put()`/文件写入抛错：**响应 500**，且**进程内存中的票数与去重标记必须回滚到写入前的状态**（不出现"返回失败但内存已计票"或"计票成功但去重标记丢失"）——具体做法：先在内存副本上应用变更、调用 `store.put()`成功后才真正提交内存状态；或捕获写入异常后撤销内存改动。重启后（或下一次成功请求前）不得看到这次失败请求造成的任何计票/去重痕迹。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2165:requirement/req-final.md:33:- **B6 前端以服务端为准 —— `voted` 字段完整契约**：
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2166:requirement/req-final.md:34:  - `GET /api/polls/:id` 的响应新增布尔字段 `voted`：请求带有效标识且该标识已投过此 poll → `true`；否则（无 cookie / 非法 cookie / 有效 cookie 但未投过）→ `false`。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2167:requirement/req-final.md:35:  - `POST /api/polls/:id/vote` 的**成功（200）**响应同样携带 `voted: true`（刚投完，视为已投）。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2168:requirement/req-final.md:36:  - `POST /api/polls/:id/vote` 的失败响应（400/403/404/409）**不新增 `voted` 字段**（沿用现有错误体 `{error}`）。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2169:requirement/req-final.md:37:  - `GET /api/admin/:token`、`POST /api/admin/:token/close` 的响应（admin view）**不包含 `voted` 字段**——admin 视图无请求方标识语境，字段无意义。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2170:requirement/req-final.md:38:  - `poll.html` 据此显示"已投过"状态（localStorage 可保留为辅助展示，但判定以服务端 `voted` 字段为准，两者冲突时以服务端为准）；服务端 4xx/409 拒绝必须以可见文案呈现给用户（`#error` 区域或等价可见元素），不得吞掉、不得静默失败。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2171:requirement/req-final.md:39:- **B7 隐私**：标识是随机匿名值，不含且不派生任何个人信息；不记录、不使用 IP；投票内容与标识的绑定仅用于防重复判定，不在任何视图/接口中暴露"谁投了什么"——公开视图（`view()`）和 admin 视图均不返回 `votes` 原始数组、`voters`/去重标记原始值、cookie 原值或标识摘要。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2172:requirement/req-final.md:41:  - poll 记录新增字段 `voters: string[]`——存储已投过该 poll 的标识的 **HMAC 摘要**（不存标识明文/签名整串，进一步降低意外泄露风险；摘要算法与 B2 签名可复用同一密钥的 HMAC，输入为标识的 `random` 部分）。
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step5-review-v1-raw.txt:2173:requirement/req-final.md:43:  - 旧 poll（无 `voters` 字段，即本次改动前创建的记录）视为 `voters: []`（没有任何标识投过），**其历史投票不追溯关联到任何标识、不清洗、不迁移**，按原样计入 `counts`/`total`；这些旧投票不会被"去重"，因为它们本来就没有关联标识——这与第 3 节 out-of-scope 的既有声明一致，此处只是把隐含的存储形态显式化。

./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:2330:**Language.** Human-facing prose — requirement docs, spec scenario descriptions, gap/design/review docs, ledger descriptions, `flow-state` notes, and every message to the human — uses the `language` field in `apriori/process-config.md`. If it is unset or `auto`, **match the language the human is using** (their kickoff and messages). Machine tokens are ALWAYS English, whatever the language: verdict lines (§5 phrase table), scenario IDs (`KV-03`), the delta keywords `ADDED`/`MODIFIED`/`REMOVED`, file paths, and this runbook. So a Chinese kickoff yields Chinese artifacts with English IDs and verdict lines — `apriori verify`/`check` keep working unchanged.
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:2343:Follow the apriori runbook (apriori/runbook.md) for change <change-name>, tier <trivial|medium|large>, track <harden|explore> (unsure: harden).
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:2349:> On the **harden** track, this kickoff (or the sign-off of the requirement doc) *is* the human intent acknowledgment — the intent card exists only on the **explore** track (§4). When the artifact root is externalized, the kickoff prompt must state it, because the flow-state file itself lives under it.
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:2361:**R1 — Stop at every human gate.** The gates are: ① STEP0 verdict at round cap ② gap-report sign-off (Large tier only) ③ STEP3 technical review ④ STEP6 KB-diff approval ⑤ any cap hit or oscillation (a reopened ledger ID). The explore track (§4) adds three **named decision points** with gate status: `intent-card sign-off`, `extraction review`, `STEP2 full review`. At a gate: update the state file, report — current step, reviewer verdict lines **verbatim**, open/rejected ledger items, the decision you need — then stop. Never approve a gate yourself; never treat "the human hasn't answered" as approval.
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:2367:**R2 — Reviews must be genuinely external.** The producing session never issues a review verdict. Spawn a heterogeneous reviewer: `codex exec -s read-only "<prompt>"` (rounds 2+: `codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <session-id> "..."` — codex CLIs ≥0.14x reject `-s` on `resume`; on older versions use `-s read-only` before the session id), or — without Codex — a **fresh** `claude` session on a different tier, fed the artifacts plus the issue ledger (P0). Paste the reviewer's verdict line back verbatim. Reviewers usually run in read-only sandboxes and cannot write the ledger: the reviewer ends its output with a **ledger delta** (new rows + status flips), and the producer lands it verbatim, marked "recorded on behalf of the reviewer"; the reviewer's raw output is archived in full at `apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*` so the recorded delta can always be diffed against its source. When invoking codex non-interactively (background/scripted), close stdin — append `< /dev/null` (PowerShell has no /dev/null: pipe instead, `$null | codex exec …`) — or it prints "Reading additional input from stdin..." and hangs. If the reviewer dies before its verdict line lands (network/provider failure mid-review), **resume the same session** and have it finish — never fill in the verdict yourself. For this to survive a *producer*-side interruption too, record the reviewer's session id in flow-state's `reviewer-session` field the moment round 1 prints it — otherwise a resume after a crash has no session to reattach to. A read-only reviewer's **dynamic observations are untrustworthy** — test runs, builds, anything needing writes can degrade inside its sandbox and produce phantom findings; only its static reads count, and the producer rejects sandbox-artifact findings with evidence from the real environment. If you cannot actually spawn a reviewer, stop and say so — **do not simulate one**.
./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:2371:**Enforcement layers** (examples, not exhaustive; the deterministic items below are *available to configure*, not active by default in this repo). Advisory text gets ignored under pressure — classify each rule by how it can be enforced: ① **deterministically enforceable now** — `process-config.md` read-only (a hook blocking agent writes), `apriori check` as a required pre-commit/CI check, `apriori verify` as the STEP5 binding gate, and the **verdict-evidence check**: every verdict line must have a matching raw archive file (`apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*`) — a mechanical backstop against simulated reviews; ② **gate-level** — Stop hooks and `/goal` conditions; ③ **inherently advisory** — a reviewer's independent judgment quality, semantic adherence to the P prompts. Reference implementation is Claude Code hooks; any CI can enforce the same checks. Example (a PreToolUse hook blocking config writes — illustrative sketch; exact schema in the Claude Code hooks docs):

./validation/legacy-lab/inherited-poll/apriori/review/vote-dedup-step2-review-v1-raw.txt:2487:**Funnel — the human decides, and the fire is carried.** "Stateable" is the human's judgment, not yours: after the approaches comparison you may *propose* exiting; only the human's approval ends the stance — and it **must funnel into the pipeline**. On approval of a stateable goal, start **STEP0**; if the goal still cannot be stated, route to the **explore track's intent card** (§4) — the same goal-certainty split as §2. There is no third resting place: brainstorm feeds one of the two. On funnel, carry everything: write the crystallized understanding as the kickoff requirement draft — goal, users, chosen approach (and the UI sketch that won, if any), success criteria, constraints, non-goals **with the reasons they were cut**, open questions — which becomes STEP0's `req-v1` starting material. Brainstorm never replaces STEP0's requirement discipline — it feeds it.

./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:30:**Language.** Human-facing prose — requirement docs, spec scenario descriptions, gap/design/review docs, ledger descriptions, `flow-state` notes, and every message to the human — uses the `language` field in `apriori/process-config.md`. If it is unset or `auto`, **match the language the human is using** (their kickoff and messages). Machine tokens are ALWAYS English, whatever the language: verdict lines (§5 phrase table), scenario IDs (`KV-03`), the delta keywords `ADDED`/`MODIFIED`/`REMOVED`, file paths, and this runbook. So a Chinese kickoff yields Chinese artifacts with English IDs and verdict lines — `apriori verify`/`check` keep working unchanged.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:41:Follow the apriori runbook (apriori/runbook.md) for change <change-name>, tier <trivial|medium|large>, track <harden|explore> (unsure: harden).
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:47:> On the **harden** track, this kickoff (or the sign-off of the requirement doc) *is* the human intent acknowledgment — the intent card exists only on the **explore** track (§4). When the artifact root is externalized, the kickoff prompt must state it, because the flow-state file itself lives under it.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:59:**R1 — Stop at every human gate.** The gates are: ① STEP0 verdict at round cap ② gap-report sign-off (Large tier only) ③ STEP3 technical review ④ STEP6 KB-diff approval ⑤ any cap hit or oscillation (a reopened ledger ID). The explore track (§4) adds three **named decision points** with gate status: `intent-card sign-off`, `extraction review`, `STEP2 full review`. At a gate: update the state file, report — current step, reviewer verdict lines **verbatim**, open/rejected ledger items, the decision you need — then stop. Never approve a gate yourself; never treat "the human hasn't answered" as approval.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:65:**R2 — Reviews must be genuinely external.** The producing session never issues a review verdict. Spawn a heterogeneous reviewer: `codex exec -s read-only "<prompt>"` (rounds 2+: `codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <session-id> "..."` — codex CLIs ≥0.14x reject `-s` on `resume`; on older versions use `-s read-only` before the session id), or — without Codex — a **fresh** `claude` session on a different tier, fed the artifacts plus the issue ledger (P0). Paste the reviewer's verdict line back verbatim. Reviewers usually run in read-only sandboxes and cannot write the ledger: the reviewer ends its output with a **ledger delta** (new rows + status flips), and the producer lands it verbatim, marked "recorded on behalf of the reviewer"; the reviewer's raw output is archived in full at `apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*` so the recorded delta can always be diffed against its source. When invoking codex non-interactively (background/scripted), close stdin — append `< /dev/null` — or it prints "Reading additional input from stdin..." and hangs. If the reviewer dies before its verdict line lands (network/provider failure mid-review), **resume the same session** and have it finish — never fill in the verdict yourself. A read-only reviewer's **dynamic observations are untrustworthy** — test runs, builds, anything needing writes can degrade inside its sandbox and produce phantom findings; only its static reads count, and the producer rejects sandbox-artifact findings with evidence from the real environment. If you cannot actually spawn a reviewer, stop and say so — **do not simulate one**.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:69:**Enforcement layers** (examples, not exhaustive; the deterministic items below are *available to configure*, not active by default in this repo). Advisory text gets ignored under pressure — classify each rule by how it can be enforced: ① **deterministically enforceable now** — `process-config.md` read-only (a hook blocking agent writes), `apriori check` as a required pre-commit/CI check, `apriori verify` as the STEP5 binding gate, and the **verdict-evidence check**: every verdict line must have a matching raw archive file (`apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*`) — a mechanical backstop against simulated reviews; ② **gate-level** — Stop hooks and `/goal` conditions; ③ **inherently advisory** — a reviewer's independent judgment quality, semantic adherence to the P prompts. Reference implementation is Claude Code hooks; any CI can enforce the same checks. Example (a PreToolUse hook blocking config writes — illustrative sketch; exact schema in the Claude Code hooks docs):
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:82:| **Trivial** | Bugfix / single file; no new user-visible behavior; no shared-state change | Light explore (facts only) → STEP5 with tests + one consistency review → STEP6 writeback if any KB fact changed |
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:94:| Neither the goal nor its acceptance can be stated | **explore** (§4's explore track) |
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:97:**Tripwires outrank the certainty axis**: anything touching external shared state / production data / module boundaries / migrations is barred from the explore track no matter how vague — run it on the harden track, optionally with a **research spike** (the STEP1 variant, §4). Default when unsure: **harden** — the opposite direction from the size axis, because the risks point the opposite way.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:108:track: harden | explore

./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:178:**Funnel — the human decides, and the fire is carried.** "Stateable" is the human's judgment, not yours: after the approaches comparison you may *propose* exiting; only the human's approval ends the stance — and it **must funnel into the pipeline**. On approval of a stateable goal, start **STEP0**; if the goal still cannot be stated, route to the **explore track's intent card** (§4) — the same goal-certainty split as §2. There is no third resting place: brainstorm feeds one of the two. On funnel, carry everything: write the crystallized understanding as the kickoff requirement draft — goal, users, chosen approach (and the UI sketch that won, if any), success criteria, constraints, non-goals **with the reasons they were cut**, open questions — which becomes STEP0's `req-v1` starting material. Brainstorm never replaces STEP0's requirement discipline — it feeds it.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:182:- **In:** `requirement/req-v{N}.md`; KB if any. The requirement must state its **target lineage** (mainline / which branch line) — in multi-lineage repos a missing lineage is a fourth interview trigger. If the requirement lacks any of the three essentials — goal / out-of-scope / testable acceptance — **interview the human first** with structured questions, then draft req-v1.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:184:- **Exit:** verdict line = `VERDICT: no major issues` → copy to `requirement/req-final.md`, advance. Cap hit → **gate ①**. Goal turns out unstateable → propose harden→explore (a human gate confirms the switch).
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:188:0. **Intent card first (non-waivable):** ≤15 lines at `requirement/intent-card.md` — goal hypothesis / success criteria / the questions the spike must answer. Requires **human sign-off** (`intent-card sign-off`; a heterogeneous review may inform it, but cannot replace it). On this track the intent card is the independent review baseline — the extracted spec is never judged against the prototype alone.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:189:1. **Spike (bounded):** prototype freely under `spike/`; cap: `spike-cap` (default 10) turns; exit = every intent-card question answered. Cap hit → **gate ⑤**.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:190:2. **P11 — spec extraction:** inputs = intent card + prototype + spike findings; outputs = spec drafts under `apriori/changes/<change>/specs/` as the **sole intent-side authority**, plus `requirement/req-final.md` as a thin index over them (§5 P11 — never a second acceptance narrative). Declared extraction-time decisions (`EXT-n`) get their final ruling at the `extraction review` decision point.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:193:5. **The prototype is disposable, machine-checkably:** STEP5 rebuilds from failing tests; tasks.md must not reference `spike/`; `spike/` is deleted (or quarantined) at archive.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll/apriori/runbook.md:194:6. **Track transitions:** explore→harden (extraction accepted, or the goal turns out clear); harden→explore (STEP0 finds the goal unstateable — via a human gate); explore→ABANDONED (hypothesis falsified). Each transition keeps the intent card, findings and ledger; only `spike/` is dropped.

The prototype is a source of observations, not of authority: where intent and prototype disagree, the intent card wins and the disagreement is listed explicitly.

./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r2/apriori/process-config.md:16:| spike-cap | 10 | ≥ 1 | 10 |
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r2/apriori/runbook.md:30:**Language.** Human-facing prose — requirement docs, spec scenario descriptions, gap/design/review docs, ledger descriptions, `flow-state` notes, and every message to the human — uses the `language` field in `apriori/process-config.md`. If it is unset or `auto`, **match the language the human is using** (their kickoff and messages). Machine tokens are ALWAYS English, whatever the language: verdict lines (§5 phrase table), scenario IDs (`KV-03`), the delta keywords `ADDED`/`MODIFIED`/`REMOVED`, file paths, and this runbook. So a Chinese kickoff yields Chinese artifacts with English IDs and verdict lines — `apriori verify`/`check` keep working unchanged.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r2/apriori/runbook.md:41:Follow the apriori runbook (apriori/runbook.md) for change <change-name>, tier <trivial|medium|large>, track <harden|explore> (unsure: harden).
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r2/apriori/runbook.md:47:> On the **harden** track, this kickoff (or the sign-off of the requirement doc) *is* the human intent acknowledgment — the intent card exists only on the **explore** track (§4). When the artifact root is externalized, the kickoff prompt must state it, because the flow-state file itself lives under it.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r2/apriori/runbook.md:59:**R1 — Stop at every human gate.** The gates are: ① STEP0 verdict at round cap ② gap-report sign-off (Large tier only) ③ STEP3 technical review ④ STEP6 KB-diff approval ⑤ any cap hit or oscillation (a reopened ledger ID). The explore track (§4) adds three **named decision points** with gate status: `intent-card sign-off`, `extraction review`, `STEP2 full review`. At a gate: update the state file, report — current step, reviewer verdict lines **verbatim**, open/rejected ledger items, the decision you need — then stop. Never approve a gate yourself; never treat "the human hasn't answered" as approval.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r2/apriori/runbook.md:65:**R2 — Reviews must be genuinely external.** The producing session never issues a review verdict. Spawn a heterogeneous reviewer: `codex exec -s read-only "<prompt>"` (rounds 2+: `codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="read-only" <session-id> "..."` — codex CLIs ≥0.14x reject `-s` on `resume`; on older versions use `-s read-only` before the session id), or — without Codex — a **fresh** `claude` session on a different tier, fed the artifacts plus the issue ledger (P0). Paste the reviewer's verdict line back verbatim. Reviewers usually run in read-only sandboxes and cannot write the ledger: the reviewer ends its output with a **ledger delta** (new rows + status flips), and the producer lands it verbatim, marked "recorded on behalf of the reviewer"; the reviewer's raw output is archived in full at `apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*` so the recorded delta can always be diffed against its source. When invoking codex non-interactively (background/scripted), close stdin — append `< /dev/null` — or it prints "Reading additional input from stdin..." and hangs. If the reviewer dies before its verdict line lands (network/provider failure mid-review), **resume the same session** and have it finish — never fill in the verdict yourself. For this to survive a *producer*-side interruption too, record the reviewer's session id in flow-state's `reviewer-session` field the moment round 1 prints it — otherwise a resume after a crash has no session to reattach to. A read-only reviewer's **dynamic observations are untrustworthy** — test runs, builds, anything needing writes can degrade inside its sandbox and produce phantom findings; only its static reads count, and the producer rejects sandbox-artifact findings with evidence from the real environment. If you cannot actually spawn a reviewer, stop and say so — **do not simulate one**.
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r2/apriori/runbook.md:69:**Enforcement layers** (examples, not exhaustive; the deterministic items below are *available to configure*, not active by default in this repo). Advisory text gets ignored under pressure — classify each rule by how it can be enforced: ① **deterministically enforceable now** — `process-config.md` read-only (a hook blocking agent writes), `apriori check` as a required pre-commit/CI check, `apriori verify` as the STEP5 binding gate, and the **verdict-evidence check**: every verdict line must have a matching raw archive file (`apriori/review/<change>-<stage>-raw.*`) — a mechanical backstop against simulated reviews; ② **gate-level** — Stop hooks and `/goal` conditions; ③ **inherently advisory** — a reviewer's independent judgment quality, semantic adherence to the P prompts. Reference implementation is Claude Code hooks; any CI can enforce the same checks. Example (a PreToolUse hook blocking config writes — illustrative sketch; exact schema in the Claude Code hooks docs):
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r2/apriori/runbook.md:82:| **Trivial** | Bugfix / single file; no new user-visible behavior; no shared-state change | Light explore (facts only) → STEP5 with tests + one consistency review → STEP6 writeback if any KB fact changed |
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r2/apriori/runbook.md:94:| Neither the goal nor its acceptance can be stated | **explore** (§4's explore track) |
./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r2/apriori/runbook.md:97:**Tripwires outrank the certainty axis**: anything touching external shared state / production data / module boundaries / migrations is barred from the explore track no matter how vague — run it on the harden track, optionally with a **research spike** (the STEP1 variant, §4). Default when unsure: **harden** — the opposite direction from the size axis, because the risks point the opposite way.

./validation/fullrun-lab/v3-poll-r2/apriori/review/quick-poll-req-review-v3-raw.jsonl:13:{"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_3","type":"command_execution","command":"/usr/bin/zsh -lc \"sed -n '1,300p' requirement/req-v3.md\"","aggregated_output":"# 需求：快速投票小工具（quick-poll）— v3\n\n> 目标线（lineage）：master（主线，空仓起步，无 merge 禁忌）\n> 变更：依 `apriori/review/quick-poll-req-review-v2.md`（STEP0·r2）修订 v2→v3，处理 REQ-1(reopen)/REQ-9/REQ-10（见文末 §10）。REQ-2..8 已 verified，不再改动。\n\n## 1. 目标（Target State B）\n\n一个零门槛的网页投票小工具。发起人填「标题 + 若干选项」建出一个投票，拿到两条链接：**投票链接**（分享给大家）和**管理链接**（只有发起人持有，用于关闭/管理）。拿到投票链接的人**无需登录、匿名**、一次点击即可投票；投完立即看到实时结果。适用于开会、群里几十人快速征集意见。\n\n## 2. 目标用户与规模\n\n- **发起人**：想快速征集意见的人（会议主持、群主、组织者）。\n- **投票人**：拿到链接的同事/朋友，**匿名、不登录**，单次投票量级约几十人（数量级 10–100，非高并发）。\n\n## 3. 数据模型与校验规则（REQ-1 / REQ-3 / REQ-4）\n\n**投票对象（poll）字段**：`pollId`、`title`、`options[]`、`mode`、`deadline?`、`status`、`counts[]`、`adminKey`。\n\n**标识符生成（REQ-3 / REQ-9）**：`pollId` 与 `adminKey` **均**由 **CSPRNG**（`crypto.randomBytes`）生成，**各 ≥128 bit 熵**，URL-safe base64 编码；**均不得从创建顺序/时间推断**（禁止递增或时间戳派生 ID）。`adminKey` 在任何日志/错误信息中不得出现。\n\n**选项归一化（REQ-1，消歧）**：\n- 每个提交的选项文本前后 **trim**；**trim 后为空 → 直接拒绝创建**（不做静默丢弃，避免\"有效选项数\"歧义）。\n- 有效选项数 = 提交的选项数，必须落在 **2..20**。\n- 允许两个选项显示文本相同（不去重文本），但每个选项由服务端分配**稳定 option ID**（`opt-0 … opt-{k-1}`，按提交顺序）。**投票提交的是 option ID，不是文本**——计票、去重、校验一律基于 option ID。\n- 单个选项文本 trim 后长度 1..200 字符。\n\n**创建请求 schema 与输入校验（REQ-4 / REQ-10）**：\n- 类型契约：`title` 必须为 string；`options` 必须为 array of string；`mode` 必须为 string；`deadline`（若提供）为 string 或 integer。**任一字段缺失、为 null 或类型错误 → 400，且不创建任何记录。**\n- `title`：trim 后长度 1..200，否则拒绝。\n- 有效选项数：**2..20**（含空选项即拒绝，见上），否则拒绝。\n- `mode` ∈ {`single`, `multiple`}，缺失或非法 → 拒绝。\n- `deadline`：可选；提供时必须是合法时间且 **晚于当前服务器时间**，否则拒绝（malformed / 过去时间均拒绝）。\n\n**管理密钥（REQ-3）**：`adminKey` 由 **CSPRNG** 生成（`crypto.randomBytes`），**≥128 bit 熵**，URL-safe base64 编码，随管理链接下发；**任何日志/错误信息中不得出现 adminKey**。\n\n## 4. 时间与截止（REQ-2）\n\n- 创建时 `deadline` 输入格式：**ISO 8601 带时区**（如 `2026-07-09T18:00:00+08:00`）或 epoch 毫秒整数；二者其一。\n- 存储：一律转为 **epoch 毫秒（UTC 基准）**。\n- 关闭判定基准：**服务器时间 `Date.now()`（UTC）**；「已到期」当且仅当 `Date.now() >= deadline`。\n- 该判定在处理请求**进入 per-poll 临界区那一刻**求值（见 §7 并发）。\n\n## 5. 核心场景（每个用户可见输出对应一个带稳定 ID 的验收项）\n\n### 建投票\n- **PC-01 创建投票**：提交合法「标题 + 2..20 有效选项 + mode + 可选 deadline」→ 创建投票，返回投票链接 + 管理链接（含 adminKey）。\n- **PC-02 创建输入校验**：违反 §3/§4 任一校验（空标题/超长、选项<2 或>20、空选项、非法 mode、malformed 或过去 deadline）→ 拒绝创建，HTTP 非 2xx，可读错误，**不创建任何投票记录**。\n\n### 投票\n- **PC-03 投票（单选）**：单选下提交**恰好一个** option ID → 该选项计数 +1，进入结果视图。\n- **PC-03b 单选多传拒绝**：单选下提交多于一个 option ID → 拒绝（非 2xx），计数不变。\n- **PC-04 投票（多选）**：多选下提交 k(k≥1) 个**互不相同**的 option ID → 各 +1；k=0（未选）→ 拒绝并提示。\n- **PC-05 浏览器软限重复投票**：同一浏览器对同一投票默认只记一次；成功投票（2xx）后本地置标记；再次打开投票链接 → 直接进入结果视图。标记存于浏览器 **localStorage**，key 作用域为 `origin + pollId`；换设备/清缓存/无痕不受本工具阻止（刻意的零门槛取舍）。\n- **PC-12 非法投票请求**：unknown `pollId` → 404；unknown option ID、重复 option ID、payload 类型错误或为空 → 400；以上任一情况**计数与状态均不变**。\n\n### 看结果\n- **PC-06 投完即看实时结果**：提交成功后进入结果视图，显示各选项票数与总票数；结果页**加载即拉取一次**，此后每 **3000ms**（`POLL_INTERVAL_MS` 常量，可配置）自动拉取最新结果（轮询，非推送）。结果 endpoint 返回 `{title, options:[{id,text,count}], total, status, deadline}`。\n\n### 关闭与生命周期\n- **PC-07 到点自动关闭**：设了 deadline 的投票，当 `Date.now() >= deadline` → 视为「已关闭」；此后投票提交返回非 2xx 并提示已结束，结果仍可读。\n- **PC-08 发起人手动关闭**：持**有效 adminKey** 者可将投票置为「已关闭」；无有效 adminKey 的关闭请求被拒绝（非 2xx），状态不变。关闭后投票提交返回非 2xx，结果仍可读。\n- **PC-09 已关闭态可见**：已关闭时，投票/结果视图显式呈现「已关闭」（含关闭原因/截止信息），不渲染投票提交控件。\n\n### 存储与并发（外部共享状态——三个时刻见 §7）\n- **PC-10 并发投票不丢票**：多名投票人几乎同时对同一投票提交合法票时，所有合法票都被计入，最终各选项计数 = 各自合法提交次数之和（无读改写交叠覆盖）。\n- **PC-11 崩溃不产生半写文件**：写入过程中进程中断，投票数据文件要么是旧完整内容、要么是新完整内容，绝不半写损坏（临时文件 + 原子 rename）。\n- **PC-13 失败不产生\"假成功\"**：create/vote/close **仅在持久化写成功后**返回 2xx；写失败（disk full、rename 失败、超时等）→ 返回非 2xx，且计数/状态/浏览器已投标记均不改变。（REQ-7）\n\n## 6. 验收标准（可测，\"若…则…\"）\n\n- PC-01：若提交合法输入，则响应含两条链接，投票链接≠管理链接，管理链接含 adminKey。\n- PC-02：若违反任一创建校验（含字段缺失/null/类型错误、空标题/超长、含空选项、选项<2 或>20、非法 mode、malformed 或过去 deadline），则非 2xx（类型/schema 错误为 400）且未创建任何投票记录。\n- PC-03 / PC-03b：单选恰好一项 → +1 并导向结果；单选传多项 → 非 2xx 且计数不变。\n- PC-04：多选传 k≥1 个不同 option ID → 各 +1；k=0 → 非 2xx。\n- PC-05：同一浏览器已投后再次访问投票链接 → 直接结果视图（localStorage 标记，作用域 origin+pollId）。\n- PC-06：投票成功 → 结果视图显示各票数与总数；加载即拉取一次，之后每 3000ms 拉取；结果响应形状如上。\n- PC-07：若 `Date.now() >= deadline`，则状态为已关闭，投票提交非 2xx，结果可读。\n- PC-08：持有效 adminKey 请求关闭 → 状态已关闭；无有效 adminKey 请求关闭 → 非 2xx 且状态不变。\n- PC-09：已关闭时视图显式呈现「已关闭」且不渲染投票控件。\n- PC-10：并发多次合法投票 → 最终各选项计数 = 各自合法提交次数之和（无丢失）。\n- PC-11：写文件过程中模拟中断 → 文件为旧完整或新完整，绝不半写损坏。\n- PC-12：unknown pollId→404；unknown/重复 option、非法/空 payload→400；计数与状态均不变。\n- PC-13：写失败时 → 非 2xx，且计数/状态/浏览器标记均不变。\n\n## 7. 外部共享状态的三个时刻（文件存储）\n\n- **初始化**：创建投票时用 CSPRNG 生成 `pollId` + `adminKey`（各 ≥128 bit，不可枚举，见 §3），落一个投票数据文件（title、options[含 id]、mode、deadline(epoch ms)、counts=0、status=open）。\n- **运行时更新**：每次 vote / close，在单一 Node 进程内用**按 pollId 串行化的写队列（per-poll lock）**执行读改写；**状态与截止判定在同一临界区内、进入临界区时求值**（REQ-6：若已 closed 或 now≥deadline → 拒绝，不计票）；写盘用**临时文件 + 原子 rename**（PC-11），rename 成功才算 durable，之后才返回 2xx（PC-13）。\n- **清理 / 失效**：v1 无自动清理（非目标）；「已关闭」为逻辑失效标志，数据仍保留可读。\n\n## 8. 技术方案（brainstorm 选定）\n\n单个 Node 服务；**文件存储（无数据库）**；服务端渲染页面 + 少量前端 JS；结果视图轮询刷新。并发安全按 §7 处理。\n\n## 9. 非目标（明确不做，附原因）\n\n- 账号 / 登录 / 注册——要的就是零门槛匿名。\n- IP 防刷票——同办公室共用出口 IP 会误伤同事。\n- 改票——避免计数回滚复杂度。\n- 建成后改标题/选项——已投票会与改后选项对不上。\n- 结果导出（CSV 等）——v1 用不上。\n- 结果可见性开关（隐藏/揭晓）——用得少，默认「投完即看」。\n- 实时推送（WebSocket）——几十人量级轮询即可。\n- 投票数据自动过期/垃圾回收——v1 不做。\n- 数据库（含 SQLite）——发起人明确不装 DB；用文件存储（并发安全由 §7 保证）。\n\n## 10. 问题处理记录（对账本）\n\nSTEP0·r1（v1→v2）：REQ-1..REQ-8 全部 accept（详见 v2 §10 各条落点）；ADV-r1 batch-acknowledged。r2 复核：REQ-2..REQ-8 → verified。\n\nSTEP0·r2（v2→v3）：\n- REQ-1 reopen → accept：§3 消歧——**trim 后为空的选项直接拒绝创建**（不静默丢弃），有效选项数=提交数∈2..20。\n- REQ-9 accept → §3/§7：`pollId` 也用 CSPRNG，≥128 bit，不可从创建顺序/时间推断。\n- REQ-10 accept → §3 创建请求类型契约（title:string / options:array of string / mode:string / deadline:string|int；缺失/null/类型错误→400 不建记录），PC-02 验收补充。\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

v1→v2，逐条处理 REQ-1..REQ-8（见文末 §10 处理记录）。\n\n## 1. 目标（Target State B）\n\n一个零门槛的网页投票小工具。发起人填「标题 + 若干选项」建出一个投票，拿到两条链接：**投票链接**（分享给大家）和**管理链接**（只有发起人持有，用于关闭/管理）。拿到投票链接的人**无需登录、匿名**、一次点击即可投票；投完立即看到实时结果。适用于开会、群里几十人快速征集意见。\n\n## 2. 目标用户与规模\n\n- **发起人**：想快速征集意见的人（会议主持、群主、组织者）。\n- **投票人**：拿到链接的同事/朋友，**匿名、不登录**，单次投票量级约几十人（数量级 10–100，非高并发）。\n\n## 3. 数据模型与校验规则（REQ-1 / REQ-3 / REQ-4）\n\n**投票对象（poll）字段**：`pollId`（服务端生成，URL-safe，不可枚举）、`title`、`options[]`、`mode`、`deadline?`、`status`、`counts[]`、`adminKey`。\n\n**选项归一化（REQ-1）**：\n- 每个选项文本提交前后 **trim**；trim 后为空的选项**无效**（不计入选项数）。\n- 允许两个选项显示文本相同（不去重文本），但每个选项由服务端分配**稳定 option ID**（`opt-0 … opt-{k-1}`，按创建顺序）。**投票提交的是 option ID，不是文本**——计票、去重、校验一律基于 option ID。\n- 单个选项文本长度 1..200 字符（trim 后）。\n\n**创建输入校验（REQ-4）**：\n- `title`：trim 后长度 1..200，否则拒绝。\n- 有效选项数：**2..20**，否则拒绝。\n- `mode` ∈ {`single`, `multiple`}，缺失或非法 → 拒绝。\n- `deadline`：可选；提供时必须是合法时间且 **晚于当前服务器时间**，否则拒绝（malformed / 过去时间均拒绝）。\n\n**管理密钥（REQ-3）**：`adminKey` 由 **CSPRNG** 生成（`crypto.randomBytes`），**≥128 bit 熵**，URL-safe base64 编码，随管理链接下发；**任何日志/错误信息中不得出现 adminKey**。\n\n## 4. 时间与截止（REQ-2）\n\n- 创建时 `deadline` 输入格式：**ISO 8601 带时区**（如 `2026-07-09T18:00:00+08:00`）或 epoch 毫秒整数；二者其一。\n- 存储：一律转为 **epoch 毫秒（UTC 基准）**。\n- 关闭判定基准：**服务器时间 `Date.now()`（UTC）**；「已到期」当且仅当 `Date.now() >= deadline`。\n- 该判定在处理请求**进入 per-poll 临界区那一刻**求值（见 §7 并发）。\n\n## 5. 核心场景（每个用户可见输出对应一个带稳定 ID 的验收项）\n\n### 建投票\n- **PC-01 创建投票**：提交合法「标题 + 2..20 有效选项 + mode + 可选 deadline」→ 创建投票，返回投票链接 + 管理链接（含 adminKey）。\n- **PC-02 创建输入校验**：违反 §3/§4 任一校验（空标题/超长、选项<2 或>20、空选项、非法 mode、malformed 或过去 deadline）→ 拒绝创建，HTTP 非 2xx，可读错误，**不创建任何投票记录**。\n\n### 投票\n- **PC-03 投票（单选）**：单选下提交**恰好一个** option ID → 该选项计数 +1，进入结果视图。\n- **PC-03b 单选多传拒绝**：单选下提交多于一个 option ID → 拒绝（非 2xx），计数不变。\n- **PC-04 投票（多选）**：多选下提交 k(k≥1) 个**互不相同**的 option ID → 各 +1；k=0（未选）→ 拒绝并提示。\n- **PC-05 浏览器软限重复投票**：同一浏览器对同一投票默认只记一次；成功投票（2xx）后本地置标记；再次打开投票链接 → 直接进入结果视图。标记存于浏览器 **localStorage**，key 作用域为 `origin + pollId`；换设备/清缓存/无痕不受本工具阻止（刻意的零门槛取舍）。\n- **PC-12 非法投票请求**：unknown `pollId` → 404；unknown option ID、重复 option ID、payload 类型错误或为空 → 400；以上任一情况**计数与状态均不变**。\n\n### 看结果\n- **PC-06 投完即看实时结果**：提交成功后进入结果视图，显示各选项票数与总票数；结果页**加载即拉取一次**，此后每 **3000ms**（`POLL_INTERVAL_MS` 常量，可配置）自动拉取最新结果（轮询，非推送）。结果 endpoint 返回 `{title, options:[{id,text,count}], total, status, deadline}`。\n\n### 关闭与生命周期\n- **PC-07 到点自动关闭**：设了 deadline 的投票，当 `Date.now() >= deadline` → 视为「已关闭」；此后投票提交返回非 2xx 并提示已结束，结果仍可读。\n- **PC-08 发起人手动关闭**：持**有效 adminKey** 者可将投票置为「已关闭」；无有效 adminKey 的关闭请求被拒绝（非 2xx），状态不变。关闭后投票提交返回非 2xx，结果仍可读。\n- **PC-09 已关闭态可见**：已关闭时，投票/结果视图显式呈现「已关闭」（含关闭原因/截止信息），不渲染投票提交控件。\n\n### 存储与并发（外部共享状态——三个时刻见 §7）\n- **PC-10 并发投票不丢票**：多名投票人几乎同时对同一投票提交合法票时，所有合法票都被计入，最终各选项计数 = 各自合法提交次数之和（无读改写交叠覆盖）。\n- **PC-11 崩溃不产生半写文件**：写入过程中进程中断，投票数据文件要么是旧完整内容、要么是新完整内容，绝不半写损坏（临时文件 + 原子 rename）。\n- **PC-13 失败不产生\"假成功\"**：create/vote/close **仅在持久化写成功后**返回 2xx；写失败（disk full、rename 失败、超时等）→ 返回非 2xx，且计数/状态/浏览器已投标记均不改变。（REQ-7）\n\n## 6. 验收标准（可测，\"若…则…\"）\n\n- PC-01：若提交合法输入，则响应含两条链接，投票链接≠管理链接，管理链接含 adminKey。\n- PC-02：若违反任一创建校验，则非 2xx 且未创建任何投票记录。\n- PC-03 / PC-03b：单选恰好一项 → +1 并导向结果；单选传多项 → 非 2xx 且计数不变。\n- PC-04：多选传 k≥1 个不同 option ID → 各 +1；k=0 → 非 2xx。\n- PC-05：同一浏览器已投后再次访问投票链接 → 直接结果视图（localStorage 标记，作用域 origin+pollId）。\n- PC-06：投票成功 → 结果视图显示各票数与总数；加载即拉取一次，之后每 3000ms 拉取；结果响应形状如上。\n- PC-07：若 `Date.now() >= deadline`，则状态为已关闭，投票提交非 2xx，结果可读。\n- PC-08：持有效 adminKey 请求关闭 → 状态已关闭；无有效 adminKey 请求关闭 → 非 2xx 且状态不变。\n- PC-09：已关闭时视图显式呈现「已关闭」且不渲染投票控件。\n- PC-10：并发多次合法投票 → 最终各选项计数 = 各自合法提交次数之和（无丢失）。\n- PC-11：写文件过程中模拟中断 → 文件为旧完整或新完整，绝不半写损坏。\n- PC-12：unknown pollId→404；unknown/重复 option、非法/空 payload→400；计数与状态均不变。\n- PC-13：写失败时 → 非 2xx，且计数/状态/浏览器标记均不变。\n\n## 7. 外部共享状态的三个时刻（文件存储）\n\n- **初始化**：创建投票时生成不可枚举 `pollId` + `adminKey`（CSPRNG），落一个投票数据文件（title、options[含 id]、mode、deadline(epoch ms)、counts=0、status=open）。\n- **运行时更新**：每次 vote / close，在单一 Node 进程内用**按 pollId 串行化的写队列（per-poll lock）**执行读改写；**状态与截止判定在同一临界区内、进入临界区时求值**（REQ-6：若已 closed 或 now≥deadline → 拒绝，不计票）；写盘用**临时文件 + 原子 rename**（PC-11），rename 成功才算 durable，之后才返回 2xx（PC-13）。\n- **清理 / 失效**：v1 无自动清理（非目标）；「已关闭」为逻辑失效标志，数据仍保留可读。\n\n## 8. 技术方案（brainstorm 选定）\n\n单个 Node 服务；**文件存储（无数据库）**；服务端渲染页面 + 少量前端 JS；结果视图轮询刷新。并发安全按 §7 处理。\n\n## 9. 非目标（明确不做，附原因）\n\n- 账号 / 登录 / 注册——要的就是零门槛匿名。\n- IP 防刷票——同办公室共用出口 IP 会误伤同事。\n- 改票——避免计数回滚复杂度。\n- 建成后改标题/选项——已投票会与改后选项对不上。\n- 结果导出（CSV 等）——v1 用不上。\n- 结果可见性开关（隐藏/揭晓）——用得少，默认「投完即看」。\n- 实时推送（WebSocket）——几十人量级轮询即可。\n- 投票数据自动过期/垃圾回收——v1 不做。\n- 数据库（含 SQLite）——发起人明确不装 DB；用文件存储（并发安全由 §7 保证）。\n\n## 10. 本轮问题处理记录（对账本，STEP0·r1）\n\n- REQ-1 accept → §3 选项归一化 + 稳定 option ID + 200 字上限。\n- REQ-2 accept → §4 时间格式/UTC 存储/比较基准。\n- REQ-3 accept → §3 CSPRNG/≥128bit/URL-safe/禁入日志。\n- REQ-4 accept → §3 创建校验（长度/2..20/mode/deadline 未来）。\n- REQ-5 accept → 新增 PC-12 非法投票请求（404/400，计数不变）。\n- REQ-6 accept → §7 临界区内判定 + 判定时点定义。\n- REQ-7 accept → 新增 PC-13 无假成功（durable 后才 2xx）。\n- REQ-8 accept → PC-06 精确 3000ms + 加载即拉一次 + endpoint 形状。\n- ADV-r1 batch-acknowledged（本地标记机制已在 PC-05 声明为 localStorage；endpoint 形状已在 PC-06 声明；其余为澄清）。\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
